# Prabowo Subianto

Prabowo Subianto

President of Indonesia since 2024

In this [Indonesian name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_name "Indonesian name"), the person is referred to by his [given name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Given_name "Given name"), _Prabowo_, and not by his [surname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surname "Surname"), _Djojohadikusumo_.

**Prabowo Subianto Djojohadikusumo**[\[a\]](#ref-2) (born 17 October 1951) is an Indonesian politician, businessman, and retired military officer who has served as the eighth [president of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Indonesia "President of Indonesia") since 2024. He previously served as the 26th [minister of defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ministers_of_Defense_(Indonesia) "List of Ministers of Defense (Indonesia)") under President [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo "Joko Widodo") from 2019 to 2024. Prabowo is Indonesia's third president to have a military background after [Suharto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suharto "Suharto") and [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono "Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono") and is the oldest first-term president in Indonesian history.

Prabowo graduated from the [Indonesian Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Military_Academy "Indonesian Military Academy") in 1970 and primarily served in the Special Forces ( [Kopassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopassus "Kopassus")) until he was appointed to lead the Strategic Reserves Command ( [Kostrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostrad "Kostrad")) in 1998. Later that same year, he was discharged from the military and subsequently banned from entering the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States") for allegedly committing human rights abuses.[\[2\]](#ref-:24)[\[3\]](#ref-4)[\[4\]](#ref-5)[\[5\]](#ref-NYT_Paddock)[\[6\]](#ref-7)

In early 2008, Prabowo's inner circle established the [Gerindra Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra_Party "Gerindra Party"). In the [2009 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Indonesian_presidential_election "2009 Indonesian presidential election"), he ran unsuccessfully for the vice presidency as [Megawati Sukarnoputri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megawati_Sukarnoputri "Megawati Sukarnoputri")'s running mate.[\[7\]](#ref-8) He contested the [2014 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_presidential_election "2014 Indonesian presidential election")[\[8\]](#ref-Prabowo_Runs_for_President-2011) and was defeated by Jakarta governor [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo "Joko Widodo"), which he initially disputed.[\[9\]](#ref-PresidentWidodo) He made another unsuccessful run for the presidency in [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indonesian_general_election "2019 Indonesian general election") against Joko Widodo, with [Sandiaga Uno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandiaga_Uno "Sandiaga Uno") as his running mate and with the support of Gerindra, the [Prosperous Justice Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosperous_Justice_Party "Prosperous Justice Party") (PKS), the [National Mandate Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Mandate_Party "National Mandate Party") (PAN), the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Indonesia) "Democratic Party (Indonesia)") (Demokrat), and [Berkarya Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkarya_Party "Berkarya Party").[\[10\]](#ref-PrabowoSandiaga)[\[11\]](#ref-12) His refusal to accept the result saw his followers stage protests that sparked [riots in Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2019_Jakarta_protests_and_riots "May 2019 Jakarta protests and riots").[\[12\]](#ref-13) However, after a heated contest, Prabowo joined Joko Widodo's [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onward_Indonesia_Cabinet "Onward Indonesia Cabinet") as his minister of defence for the 2019 to 2024 period.[\[13\]](#ref-14)

On 10 October 2021, Gerindra announced Prabowo as their candidate in the [2024 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_general_election "2024 Indonesian general election").[\[14\]](#ref-15) On 12 August 2022, Prabowo announced that he accepted Gerindra's nomination.[\[15\]](#ref-16) Prabowo declared victory in the election on 14 February 2024, as early unofficial polling showed him with a lead in the first round of voting.[\[16\]](#ref-17) On 20 March, the General Election Commission (KPU) certified the results and declared him as the [president-elect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_elect "President elect") of Indonesia.[\[17\]](#ref-:12) The [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia "Constitutional Court of Indonesia") (MK) confirmed his status on 22 April 2024.[\[18\]](#ref-19)[\[19\]](#ref-20)[\[20\]](#ref-21) Prabowo was [sworn in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Prabowo_Subianto "Inauguration of Prabowo Subianto") as the 8th president of Indonesia on 20 October 2024.

As president, Prabowo established the [Red and White Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_and_White_Cabinet "Red and White Cabinet"), the largest in Indonesia's modern history. He rolled out a series of economic reforms, launched the sovereign wealth fund [Danantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danantara "Danantara"), and signed several defence agreements and acquisitions. His flagship [free school meals programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Nutritious_Meals "Free Nutritious Meals") attracted controversy due to mass food poisonings. He oversaw Indonesia's entry to [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS").

## Early life and family

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b9/RM_Margono_Djojohadikusumo.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:RM_Margono_Djojohadikusumo.jpg) Prabowo at 12 years old (standing right), with his siblings and grandparents, [Margono Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margono_Djojohadikusumo "Margono Djojohadikusumo") and Siti Katoemi Wirodihardjo

Prabowo's father, [Sumitro Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumitro_Djojohadikusumo "Sumitro Djojohadikusumo") (1917–2001), was originally from [Gombong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gombong "Gombong"), [Kebumen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kebumen_Regency "Kebumen Regency"). He was an economist who served as former President [Sukarno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukarno "Sukarno")'s minister for the economy and [Suharto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suharto "Suharto")'s [minister for research and technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Research_and_Technology/National_Research_and_Innovation_Agency_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Research and Technology/National Research and Innovation Agency (Indonesia)").[\[21\]](#ref-22) Sumitro named Prabowo after his own younger brother who was killed in an incident against [Japanese forces in Lengkong, Tangerang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lengkong_incident "Lengkong incident") during the [Indonesian National Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Revolution "Indonesian National Revolution").[\[22\]](#ref-Friend) Prabowo's mother, [Dora Marie Sigar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dora_Marie_Sigar "Dora Marie Sigar") (1919–2008), was a [Protestant Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_Christian "Protestant Christian") of [Minahasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minahasan_people "Minahasan people") and [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Indonesians "German Indonesians") descent, who originated from the Sigar-Maengkom family in Langowan, [North Sulawesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sulawesi "North Sulawesi").[\[23\]](#ref-24) Her father was a member of the [Volksraad of the Dutch East Indies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volksraad_(Dutch_East_Indies) "Volksraad (Dutch East Indies)"). They were married in [Matraman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matraman "Matraman"), [East Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Jakarta "East Jakarta").[\[24\]](#ref-kumparan)

Prabowo has two older sisters, Bianti Djojohadikusumo, who was born in 1946 and Maryani Djojohadikusumo, who was born in 1948.[\[24\]](#ref-kumparan) His only brother, [Hashim Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashim_Djojohadikusumo "Hashim Djojohadikusumo"), was born in 1953.[\[24\]](#ref-kumparan) Prabowo's eldest sister, Bianti, is married to [J. Soedradjad Djiwandono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Soedradjad_Djiwandono "J. Soedradjad Djiwandono"), Governor of Bank Indonesia from 1993 until 1998, whilst her sister, Maryani, was married to a French expatriate entrepreneur, Didier Lemaistre who died in 2018.[\[24\]](#ref-kumparan) Prabowo's brother, Hashim, is one of the wealthiest businessmen in Indonesia with assets across the globe from Indonesia to Europe and North America. Due to his father's political exile borne from differences with Sukarno in the 1960s, they lived in self-exile, notably in countries such as Switzerland, Singapore, Thailand, Malaysia, and the United Kingdom.[\[25\]](#ref-26) During his high school years, he studied at [Victoria Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Institution "Victoria Institution") in [Kuala Lumpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuala_Lumpur "Kuala Lumpur"), Malaysia.[\[26\]](#ref-27) Between 1966 and 1968, the family lived in London, where Prabowo attended and graduated from the [American School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_School_in_London "American School in London").[\[27\]](#ref-28)[\[28\]](#ref-29) Sumitro subsequently encouraged his son to attend a military academy. According to peers and observers, Prabowo was strategic and had an appetite for political power.[\[22\]](#ref-Friend)

Sumitro himself came from a political background. His father, [Margono Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margono_Djojohadikusumo "Margono Djojohadikusumo"), was the founder of the country's first state bank [Bank Negara Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Negara_Indonesia "Bank Negara Indonesia") (BNI), the first leader of Indonesia's provisional [Supreme Advisory Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Advisory_Council "Supreme Advisory Council") (DPA), and a member of the [Investigating Committee for Preparatory Work for Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investigating_Committee_for_Preparatory_Work_for_Independence "Investigating Committee for Preparatory Work for Independence") (BPUPK) that drafted the steps for [Indonesia's independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_Indonesian_Independence "Proclamation of Indonesian Independence").[\[29\]](#ref-30) Prabowo is the third generation of a family that has served political positions in Indonesia since its independence in 1945.

Prabowo and Titiek have a son, [Didit Hediprasetyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didit_Hediprasetyo "Didit Hediprasetyo"), who lived in [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston "Boston") before settling in Paris to pursue a career in fashion design. While his son has shown little interest in politics, Prabowo is an uncle to Gerindra politicians [Thomas Djiwandono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Djiwandono "Thomas Djiwandono"), [Budi Djiwandono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budi_Djiwandono "Budi Djiwandono"), [Aryo Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryo_Djojohadikusumo "Aryo Djojohadikusumo"), and [Rahayu Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahayu_Saraswati "Rahayu Saraswati"), who will together continue the Djojohadikusumo political lineage.[\[30\]](#ref-31)

## Military career

### Military academy and service

See also: [Indonesian occupation of East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_occupation_of_East_Timor "Indonesian occupation of East Timor") and [Papua conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papua_conflict "Papua conflict")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/61/Danjen_Kopassus_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Danjen_Kopassus_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg) Prabowo as Commandant General of [Kopassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopassus "Kopassus")

Prabowo enrolled in the [Indonesian Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Military_Academy "Indonesian Military Academy") (_Akademi Militer Nasional_) in [Magelang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magelang "Magelang") in 1970.[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003) He graduated in 1974 with others who would gain senior leadership positions, such as [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono "Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono").[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003)

In 1976, Prabowo served in the [Indonesian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Army "Indonesian Army") Special Forces Command ( [Kopassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopassus "Kopassus")) and was assigned as the commander of Group 1 _Komando Pasukan Sandhi Yudha_ (Kopassandha), which was one of the Indonesian Army's Nanggala commando units in [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Timor "East Timor"), the former [Portuguese territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Timor "Portuguese Timor") that [Indonesia had invaded the previous year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_invasion_of_East_Timor "Indonesian invasion of East Timor").[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003) Prabowo, then 26 years old, was the youngest Nanggala commander.[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003) Prabowo led the mission to capture the vice president of [Fretilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fretilin "Fretilin"), who was the first prime minister of East Timor, [Nicolau dos Reis Lobato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolau_dos_Reis_Lobato "Nicolau dos Reis Lobato").[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003) Guiding Prabowo was Antonio Lobato – Nicolau's younger brother. On 31 December 1978, Prabowo's company found and fatally shot Nicolau in the stomach as he was being escorted in [Maubisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maubisse "Maubisse"), fifty kilometres south of [Dili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dili "Dili").[\[31\]](#ref-Conboy,_Ken_2003)

In 1981, Captain Inf. Prabowo with Major Inf. [Luhut Binsar Panjaitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhut_Binsar_Pandjaitan "Luhut Binsar Pandjaitan") was sent by the TNI to attend Anti-terror Training in [GSG-9 Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSG_9 "GSG 9"), and after graduate from the course, TNI forming the first anti-terror unit in Indonesia, the Detasemen 81 Anti Teror under the Kopassus, with Luhut as the detachment commander and Prabowo as the vice-commander.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

In 1983, sources say that Prabowo commanded the special forces responsible for the [Kraras massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraras_massacre "Kraras massacre") in East Timor.[\[32\]](#ref-Santoso) The survivors of these massacres were locked up in a concentration camp guarded by Prabowo's men, where many died of starvation and ill-treatment.[\[33\]](#ref-:17) Prabowo claimed he was nowhere near the Viqueque district when the massacre happened, and neither the UN nor the government of Timor Leste have ever proffered charges of human rights violations against him.[\[34\]](#ref-pbw) Jose Manuel Tesoro, writing for _Asiaweek_ in 2000, contacted four separate non-governmental organisations including TAPOL in London; Solidamor in Jakarta; the HAK Foundation, headquartered in Dili; and the East Timor Action Network (ETAN) in New York, and they could not provide any eyewitness reports, transcripts of intercepted communications, leaked papers, or anything that could substantiate Prabowo's involvement.[\[35\]](#ref-jmt)[\[32\]](#ref-Santoso)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Prabowo_in_East_Timor.jpg/500px-Prabowo_in_East_Timor.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prabowo_in_East_Timor.jpg) Indonesian Army's Nanggala commando unit in [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Timor "East Timor") led by Prabowo

In 1985 Prabowo attended the Advanced Infantry Officers Course at [Fort Bragg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Bragg "Fort Bragg"), in the United States for commando training.[\[36\]](#ref-37) In the early 1990s, as the commander of Kopassus Group 3, the now Major General Prabowo attempted to crush the [East Timorese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Timor "East Timor") independence movement by using irregular troops (hooded "ninja" gangs dressed in black and operating at night) and, in main towns and villages, militias trained and directed by Kopassus commanders. Human rights abuses rose. The Army's 1997 campaign was called _Operation Eradicate_.[\[37\]](#ref-38)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Letjen_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg/500px-Letjen_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Letjen_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg) Prabowo as Chief of the Indonesian Armed Forces Command and Staff School (Sesko ABRI) in his uniform with multiple decorations

In 1996, Prabowo led [Operation Mapenduma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapenduma_hostage_crisis "Mapenduma hostage crisis") in the mountainous terrain of [Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papua_(province) "Papua (province)"). The goal of the operation was the release of 11 scientific researchers who had been taken hostage by the [Free Papua Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Papua_Movement "Free Papua Movement") (OPM). The researchers were five Indonesians, four Britons, one Dutchman and his pregnant German wife. Two of the Indonesian male hostages were killed shortly before the rescue operation. The mission involved covert support from British Military Attaché and [SAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Air_Service "Special Air Service") veteran Colonel Ivor Helberg.[\[38\]](#ref-39) Following the hostage transfer, Kopassus under Prabowo began a reprisal campaign against villages perceived to support OPM, and, in one incident at Geselema village, attacking the villagers with a military helicopter disguised as a [Red Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Cross "Red Cross") helicopter.[\[39\]](#ref-40)

On 20 March 1998, Prabowo was appointed head of the 27,000-strong Army Strategic Reserve Command ( [Kostrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostrad "Kostrad")), that Suharto had commanded in 1965.[\[40\]](#ref-41) During his military career, he received the nicknames "08" and "Pandu" (lit.'Scout') from the Kopassus and the Kostrad, respectively.[\[41\]](#ref-42)

### Role in the fall of Suharto

Main article: [Fall of Suharto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Suharto "Fall of Suharto")

Less than three months after his appointment as head of Kostrad, on the first day of the [May 1998 riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_1998_riots "May 1998 riots"), Prabowo urged the commander of the [Indonesian National Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces "Indonesian National Armed Forces") (ABRI), General [Wiranto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiranto "Wiranto"), to let him bring his Strategic Reserve units from outside Jakarta into the city to help restore order.[\[42\]](#ref-43) Hundreds of men trained by Kopassus (Prabowo's former command) were flown from [Dili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dili "Dili") to [Yogyakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yogyakarta "Yogyakarta") in chartered planes, and then on to Jakarta by train.[\[43\]](#ref-Friend_2003,_p._331) Prabowo publicly urged Indonesians to join him to fight "traitors to the nation".[\[44\]](#ref-Friend_2003,_p._315) On the morning of 14 May, Kopassus troops escorted young thugs from [Lampung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lampung "Lampung") in southern Sumatra into the capital.[\[45\]](#ref-46) Thus Prabowo was accused of using his contacts in his former command to import and create trouble, while Wiranto had declined to give Prabowo's current command, Kostrad, permission to quell the existing trouble, in line with classic Javanese tactic to stir chaos to discredit a rival and/or seize power.[\[43\]](#ref-Friend_2003,_p._331)

Troops under Prabowo's command kidnapped and tortured at least nine democracy activists in the months before the [May 1998 riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_1998_riots_of_Indonesia "May 1998 riots of Indonesia").[\[46\]](#ref-47) In one testimony from Andi Arief, a former detainee told of being tortured for days in an unidentified location, allegedly a military camp where most of their time was spent blindfolded, while being forced to answer repeated questions, mainly concerning their political activities. The abuse included being punched, terrorised physically and mentally, and given electric shocks.[\[47\]](#ref-48) Later, in 2009, two of the nine men were candidates for [Gerindra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra_Party "Gerindra Party"), Prabowo's political party, and another served as his media adviser.[\[48\]](#ref-49) By 2024, six out of the nine kidnapped (including Arief) have either worked for Prabowo, or backed him for the presidency.[\[49\]](#ref-1BBC2024) Prabowo was also suspected of organising the [kidnappings of another 13 activists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997%E2%80%9398_activists_kidnappings_in_Indonesia "1997–98 activists kidnappings in Indonesia") (all of whom remain "missing") between February 1997 and May 1998.[\[50\]](#ref-51)

Later investigations into the May riots revealed that violence in Jakarta was the result of an internal struggle within the military elite to become Suharto's successor.[\[51\]](#ref-52) Many believed Prabowo, as Army Strategic Reserve Command (Kostrad) commander, sought to become his father-in-law's successor and coveted the commander of the Armed Forces position held by General Wiranto, who was favoured to succeed Suharto. Together with Operations Commander for Greater Jakarta (_Panglima Komando Operasi Jakarta Raya_, Pangkoops Jaya) Major General [Sjafrie Sjamsoeddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sjafrie_Sjamsoeddin "Sjafrie Sjamsoeddin"), Prabowo aimed to terrorise opponents of the government and to show that Wiranto was "an incompetent commander who could not control disorder".[\[52\]](#ref-JP2)[\[53\]](#ref-Purdey_107) During August and September, the fact-finding team interviewed Prabowo, Sjafrie, and other military commanders regarding their movements during the Jakarta riots. Prabowo asserted that he was unsure of the precise movements of military forces in the capital and deferred to Sjafrie.[\[54\]](#ref-55) In its final report, the fact-finding team suspected that, on the night of 14 May, Prabowo met with several Armed Forces and prominent civilian figures at the Kostrad headquarters to discuss the organisation of the violence.[\[55\]](#ref-Purdey_148) However, this was later refuted by several people who attended the meeting, including prominent human rights lawyer [Adnan Buyung Nasution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adnan_Buyung_Nasution "Adnan Buyung Nasution") and Joint Fact-Finding Team member, [Bambang Widjojanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambang_Widjojanto "Bambang Widjojanto").[\[56\]](#ref-57) Further testimonies by Prabowo[\[57\]](#ref-58) in the years following the investigation contradicted the team's report and led to scepticism of the team's allegations.[\[58\]](#ref-59)

### Dismissal from the military

Main article: [1998 Indonesian coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Indonesian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt "1998 Indonesian coup d'état attempt")

On 21 May 1998, Suharto announced his resignation from the presidency and Vice President [B. J. Habibie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._J._Habibie "B. J. Habibie") took over as president. On the afternoon following Habibie's inauguration as president, Prabowo demanded of Habibie that he be put in charge of the army in place of Wiranto. However, Habibie and Wiranto actually dismissed Prabowo from his position as Kostrad commander, and the following day, announced Wiranto's appointment as minister of defence and security as well as commander of the [Indonesian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Army "Indonesian Army"), which resulted in Prabowo's dismissal. There were two versions of how this happened: the first version states that a furious Prabowo went to the Presidential Palace carrying a gun and a truck with his Kostrad troops. Because he was prevented from entering Habibie's office, he went to Suharto, who instead reprimanded him.[\[59\]](#ref-60)[\[60\]](#ref-61)[\[61\]](#ref-62) Meanwhile, another version states that Prabowo was dismissed from his post because he was suspected of carrying out a coup against Habibie.[\[62\]](#ref-63) It is said that after Habibie's inauguration, Wiranto reported that there was movement from Kostrad troops appearing around Habibie's residence. Habibie then ordered Prabowo to be dismissed from his post just before sunset in that day.[\[63\]](#ref-64)[\[64\]](#ref-65)[\[65\]](#ref-66) However, Prabowo was later visited by Wiranto at his home on the weekend of 23 or 24 May and then reassigned to a non-combat role at the Armed Forces Command and General Staff College in [Bandung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandung "Bandung").[\[66\]](#ref-67)

Following an ABRI investigation in August 1998, the _Dewan Kehormatan Perwira_ (Officers Council of Honour) found Prabowo guilty of "misinterpreting orders" in the kidnapping of nine anti-Suharto activists in 1998.[\[67\]](#ref-www-cgi.cnn.com) Prabowo acknowledged responsibility for the kidnapping of the nine activists,[\[68\]](#ref-Friend_a) and was discharged honourably from military service in November.[\[69\]](#ref-keppres) He and Wiranto denied that the discharge was a result of disciplinary action.[\[70\]](#ref-Purdey_2006_155) Later he went into voluntary exile in [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan "Jordan")[\[70\]](#ref-Purdey_2006_155) where he knew that country's new young King Abdullah as a fellow commander of special forces.[\[68\]](#ref-Friend_a) In an interview with _[Asiaweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asiaweek "Asiaweek")_ magazine in 2000, Prabowo said "I never threatened Habibie. I was not behind the riots. That is a great lie. I never betrayed Suharto. I never betrayed Habibie. I never betrayed my country. There was a certain group that wanted to make me a scapegoat, maybe to hide their involvement."[\[67\]](#ref-www-cgi.cnn.com) Human rights groups have long questioned Prabowo's eligibility to run for president, noting that he was discharged from the Army in August 1998[\[b\]](#ref-72) for "misinterpreting orders" in the abduction of the democracy activists. While that was the military's official statement, observers have long believed that it was a coup conspiracy that saw Prabowo, then the commander of the Army Strategic Reserves, given his marching orders.[\[71\]](#ref-73)

As a [2014 presidential candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_presidential_election#Political_parties "2014 Indonesian presidential election"), Prabowo's past came under renewed scrutiny, with many organisations calling for him to step down. On 19 April, he was criticised by a poet made by Fahmi Habcyi which linked him to the disappearance of Widji Thukul and urged him to return Thukul as his wife is devastated hoping for her husband to return.[\[72\]](#ref-74) A coalition, which consisted of Imparsial, Kontras, the Setara Institute, and the Human Rights Working Group (HRWG), combined under the Civil Society Coalition Against Forgetting, visited the [National Commission on Human Rights (Komnas HAM)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Commission_on_Human_Rights_(Indonesia) "National Commission on Human Rights (Indonesia)") in Jakarta on 7 May 2014 to urge the commission to re-investigate Prabowo.[\[73\]](#ref-Prabowo_unsuitable_for_president,_rights_campaigners_say) A 27 June 2014 report indicated that an investigative journalist, [Allan Nairn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_Nairn "Allan Nairn"), had been threatened with arrest "for revealing the former general's role in human rights abuses."[\[74\]](#ref-76)

During the 2024 presidential debate on 12 December 2023, Prabowo admitted implicitly that he had [Budiman Sudjatmiko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budiman_Sudjatmiko "Budiman Sudjatmiko") kidnapped, a violation of human rights, later Sudjatmiko had become one of his spokesperson.[\[75\]](#ref-77) Prior to the debate, Prabowo also admitted to Sudjatmiko, that he had released all activists he had kidnapped but didn't know of their later fates after released.[\[76\]](#ref-78) Explaining his decision, Budiman claimed people changed after 25 years, both him and Prabowo "moved to the middle."[\[49\]](#ref-1BBC2024) [Maria Catarina Sumarsih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Catarina_Sumarsih?action=edit&redlink=1 "Maria Catarina Sumarsih"), whose son was shot dead during the [Semanggi shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semanggi_shootings "Semanggi shootings") on 13 November 1998, said that Prabowo was responsible for the tragedy.[\[77\]](#ref-79)

## Business career

After being discharged from the military, Prabowo joined his brother [Hashim Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashim_Djojohadikusumo "Hashim Djojohadikusumo")'s business. He purchased Kiani Kertas, a paper pulp and plantation company based in Mangkajang, [East Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Kalimantan "East Kalimantan").[\[78\]](#ref-watchindonesia.org) Prior to Prabowo's purchase, Kiani was owned by [Bob Hasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hasan "Bob Hasan"), a businessman close to former President [Suharto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soeharto "Soeharto").[\[78\]](#ref-watchindonesia.org) Today, Prabowo's Nusantara Group controls 27 companies in Indonesia and abroad. Prabowo's companies include Nusantara Energy ( [oil and natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Indonesia "Energy in Indonesia"), coal), Tidar Kerinci Agung ( [palm oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_oil_production_in_Indonesia "Palm oil production in Indonesia") plantations) and Jaladri Nusantara (fishery industry).[\[79\]](#ref-81) Prabowo rebranded Kiani Kertas to Kertas Nusantara. The company was established in 1990 and is part of the Nusantara Energy. It controls an area of 3,400 hectares (8,400 acres) used for paper mills, employee housing, private schools, and various company facilities.[\[80\]](#ref-82) Kiani has been awarded ISO 900–2005 status as one of the highest quality management companies. It is reported that Kiani Kertas has been experiencing financial difficulties, and in early 2014, workers took to the streets to demand their wages which had not been paid in five months.[\[81\]](#ref-83)

Prabowo was the wealthiest presidential candidate in the 2009 election, with assets of Rp 1.5trillion (about US$150million) and US$7.5million.[\[82\]](#ref-84)

In 2007, PT Ridlatama, whose majority stakeholder was British-based Churchill PLC, conducted a geo-survey of [eastern Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Kalimantan "East Kalimantan") for [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_coal_production "List of countries by coal production"). Two months after the survey yielded positive results, East Kutai officials granted mining licenses to Nusantara Energy (a subsidiary of the Nusantara Group, a conglomerate owned by Prabowo's family) to operate in the area surveyed by Ridlatama. In 2010, Ridlatama's license was revoked, effectively completing Nusantara's take over of Churchill's operations. Churchill appealed to the Supreme Court of Indonesia but lost the case. In 2012, Churchill filed a case against the government of Indonesia at the [International Centre for Settlement of Investment Disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Centre_for_Settlement_of_Investment_Disputes "International Centre for Settlement of Investment Disputes"), demanding US$2billion in compensation. Indonesia argued that ICSID had no authority to arbitrate. In 2014, ICSID ruled that it had the authority and the case is still ongoing.[\[83\]](#ref-85)[\[84\]](#ref-86)[\[85\]](#ref-87)[\[86\]](#ref-88)

In 2014, the regent of East Kutai, [Isran Noor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isran_Noor "Isran Noor"), publicly endorsed Prabowo as a presidential candidate.[\[87\]](#ref-89) He also considered pressing criminal charges against Churchill, alleging that Churchill forged its license.[\[88\]](#ref-90)

## Advocacy career

### Farmers' rights (HKTI)

The Indonesian Farmers' Association (HKTI) was established in 1973 to advocate for the farmers' rights. Prabowo was elected President of HKTI on 5 December 2004,[\[89\]](#ref-91)[\[90\]](#ref-92) and he was reappointed in 2010 for a second term.[\[91\]](#ref-93)

### Market traders (APPSI)

The Indonesian Traditional Market Traders Association (APPSI) is a non-profit organisation advocating for the welfare of traders in Indonesia's traditional markets. Prabowo was elected as president of APPSI in 2008.[\[92\]](#ref-94) As a chairman of the organisation, Prabowo often calls for the government to limit hypermarkets by regulating their distances so as not to harm small traders. "So far, traditional market traders have always been ignored, so that when a modern market was established, the capital owners of the market traders had to be willing to be disbanded due to demolition," said Prabowo.[\[93\]](#ref-95)

### _Pencak silat_ and the IPSI

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Prabowo_and_Indonesian_Pencak_Silat_Association.jpg/500px-Prabowo_and_Indonesian_Pencak_Silat_Association.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prabowo_and_Indonesian_Pencak_Silat_Association.jpg) Prabowo opening the 2011 _pencak silat_ [SEA Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEA_Games "SEA Games") tournament held in [Taman Mini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taman_Mini "Taman Mini"), [Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta "Jakarta"). He is the chairman of Indonesia's _pencak silat_ organisation, the Indonesian Pencak Silat Association.

_[Pencak silat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pencak_silat "Pencak silat")_ is one of Indonesia's traditional martial arts. The Indonesian Pencak Silat Association (IPSI) oversees the regulation of the sport in Indonesia, develops athletes, and organises tournaments. Prabowo was elected as president of the IPSI in 2004 and was re-elected in 2012 for a third consecutive term.[\[94\]](#ref-96)

At the [2011 SEA Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_SEA_Games "2011 SEA Games") in Jakarta, _pencak silat_ won the overall championship by winning 9 of the 18 events competed.[\[95\]](#ref-97) The IPSI's achievements under Prabowo's chairmanship were noted at the [2018 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Asian_Games "2018 Asian Games"), where _pencak silat_ succeeded in contributing to 14 of the 31 gold medals won by the Indonesian contingent.

Prabowo is also the president of the [International Pencak Silat Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Pencak_Silat_Federation "International Pencak Silat Federation") (IPSF).[\[96\]](#ref-98)

### Migrant workers

The Indonesian Advocacy Service for Justice and Peace (Padma) in the [East Nusa Tenggara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Nusa_Tenggara "East Nusa Tenggara") region assessed that Prabowo played a major role in the release of Wilfrida Soik from the death penalty at the Kota Bahru Court, [Kelantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelantan "Kelantan"), Malaysia.[\[97\]](#ref-99) Prabowo appointed Malaysian lawyer Tan Sri Mohammad Syafei to defend Wilfrida Soik. Wilfrida is a worker from East Nusa Tenggara who was sent illegally. Wilfrida was sentenced to death for killing her employer, Yeap Seok Pen, on 7 December 2010.[\[98\]](#ref-100)

## Early political career

Using his connections to President Suharto, Prabowo and his brother worked to silence journalistic and political critics in the 1990s. Hashim unsuccessfully pressured [Goenawan Mohamad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goenawan_Mohamad "Goenawan Mohamad") to sell his outspoken and banned _[Tempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempo_(Indonesia_magazine) "Tempo (Indonesia magazine)")_ magazine to him.[\[99\]](#ref-101) As a lieutenant colonel, Prabowo invited [Abdurrahman Wahid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdurrahman_Wahid "Abdurrahman Wahid") to his battalion headquarters in 1992 and warned him to stick to religion and to stay out of politics, or face unspecified actions if he continued to oppose the president.[\[100\]](#ref-102) He later warned the intellectual [Nurcholish Madjid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurcholish_Madjid "Nurcholish Madjid") (Cak Nur) to resign from the KIPP, the election monitoring unit set up by Goenawan Mohamad, which armed forces commander [Feisal Tanjung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feisal_Tanjung "Feisal Tanjung") had denounced as "obviously unconstitutional."[\[101\]](#ref-103)

### Golkar Party convention

In 2004, Prabowo was one of five contenders in the [2004 Golkar Party National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Golkar_Party_National_Convention?action=edit&redlink=1 "2004 Golkar Party National Convention")\[ [id](https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konvensi%20Calon%20Presiden%20Partai%20Golongan%20Karya "id:Konvensi Calon Presiden Partai Golongan Karya")\] vying to become [Golkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golkar "Golkar") party's presidential candidate. He received the lowest number of votes, just 39, and was eliminated in the first round.[\[102\]](#ref-104) The second round of voting was won by [Wiranto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiranto "Wiranto"). After finishing last in the party convention, Prabowo served as a member of the Golkar Advisory Council until his resignation on 12 July 2008.

### Gerindra Party

In February 2008, Prabowo's inner circle, including [Fadli Zon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fadli_Zon "Fadli Zon"), [Muchdi Purwopranjono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muchdi_Purwopranjono "Muchdi Purwopranjono"), and Prabowo's younger brother [Hashim Djojohadikusumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashim_Djojohadikusumo "Hashim Djojohadikusumo"), along with a few others established the [Gerindra Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra_Party "Gerindra Party"). Prabowo served as chairman of the Board of Trustees of the Central Leadership Council (DPP). He was later appointed general chairman of the party after the death of the party's previous chairman Suhardi in 2014.[\[103\]](#ref-105)

## Vice-presidential candidacy

See also: [2009 Indonesian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Indonesian_presidential_election "2009 Indonesian presidential election")

In May 2009, Gerindra nominated Prabowo for the presidency in the [2009 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Indonesian_presidential_election "2009 Indonesian presidential election").[\[104\]](#ref-106) However, having won 26 out of 560 seats in the Indonesian parliament, the party did not have the required numbers, and Prabowo ran as a vice-presidential candidate to [Megawati Sukarnoputri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megawati_Sukarnoputri "Megawati Sukarnoputri"), daughter of Indonesia's first president [Sukarno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukarno "Sukarno"). Prabowo and Megawati signed the Batu Tulis Agreement, which stated that:[\[105\]](#ref-107)

1. [PDI-P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle "Indonesian Democratic Party of Struggle") and Gerindra nominated Megawati as presidential candidate and Prabowo as vice-presidential candidate in the 2009 elections.
2. If elected, Prabowo can control Indonesia's economic programs and policies which are "based on the principles of standing on one's own feet, being sovereign in the political field, and having a national personality in the cultural field within the framework of a presidential system"
3. Prabowo can determine who will be Minister of Forestry, Minister of Agriculture, Minister of Finance, Minister of State Owned Enterprises, Minister of Energy and Mineral Resources, Minister of Maritime Affairs and Fisheries, Minister of Industry, Minister of Manpower and Transmigration, Minister of Law and Human Rights, and Minister of Defence.
4. The government that is formed will support the PDI-P's people's program and the Gerindra's eight action programs for the prosperity of the people.
5. Funding for the 2009 elections will be covered 50% by Megawati and 50% by Prabowo.
6. Megawati shall back Prabowo as 2014 presidential election candidate.

The pair, referred to colloquially by the Indonesian media as _Mega–Pro_, earned 27% of the vote and lost to [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono "Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono") and his running mate, economist [Boediono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boediono "Boediono").[\[106\]](#ref-108) The results of the KPU's manual calculation, which were announced on 25 July 2009, were not much different from the results of the [quick count](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quick_count "Quick count"). Megawati and Prabowo were absent at the announcement of the official tally even though Law No. 42 of 2008 concerning Presidential and Vice-Presidential Elections mandated each pair of candidates to be present in determining the official results of the presidential election.[\[107\]](#ref-109)

## Presidential candidacies

### 2014 general election

See also: [2014 Indonesian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_presidential_election "2014 Indonesian presidential election")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Prabowo_Accepts_Gerindra%27s_Nomination.jpg/500px-Prabowo_Accepts_Gerindra%27s_Nomination.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prabowo_Accepts_Gerindra's_Nomination.jpg) Prabowo accepting the [Gerindra Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra_Party "Gerindra Party")'s nomination rally for the 2014 presidential election in Lembah Hambalang on 17 March 2012[\[108\]](#ref-republika.co.id)

In November 2011, Prabowo announced his intention to run in the [2014 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_presidential_election "2014 Indonesian presidential election").[\[8\]](#ref-Prabowo_Runs_for_President-2011) Surveys published by the Center for Policy Studies and Strategic Development (Puskaptis)[\[109\]](#ref-111) and by the Indonesian Survey Institute published on 23 February 2012[\[110\]](#ref-112) gave him the lead – but observers and activists cast doubt on the polls.[\[111\]](#ref-113)

In March 2012, Gerindra named Prabowo its 2014 presidential candidate.[\[108\]](#ref-republika.co.id) The party's slogan was then changed to _Gerindra Menang Prabowo Presiden_ (Gerindra Wins, Prabowo Becomes President).[\[112\]](#ref-114) Prabowo said he would run an investment-friendly administration if he won and that Indonesia needed more energy exploration. Furthermore, he said he had been in close contact with labour unions and believed rising worker discontent could be managed with a wise national budget.[\[113\]](#ref-115) He promised to use military-style efficiency to push through chronically delayed infrastructure projects, as well as to create jobs in the archipelago's backwaters by improving agricultural productivity.[\[114\]](#ref-Eric_Bellman) Another pillar to Prabowo's platform was that he was solidly secular, and his party planned to protect the rights of minority religious groups in the Muslim-majority country.[\[114\]](#ref-Eric_Bellman)

According to numerous quick counts after [the 9 April legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_legislative_election "2014 Indonesian legislative election"), Gerinda came in third place, positioning Prabowo as one of two leading presidential candidates for the election to be held 9 July, the other being Jakarta governor, [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo "Joko Widodo").[\[115\]](#ref-117) On Tuesday, 20 May 2014, [Golkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golkar "Golkar"), along with the [United Development Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Development_Party "United Development Party") (PPP), the [National Mandate Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Mandate_Party "National Mandate Party") (PAN), the [Prosperous Justice Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosperous_Justice_Party "Prosperous Justice Party") (PKS), and the [Crescent Star Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_Star_Party_(Indonesia) "Crescent Star Party (Indonesia)") (PBB), officially endorsed Prabowo to run for the 2014 presidential election; the coalition collected 48.9% of votes and 52.1 seats in the parliament.[\[116\]](#ref-118) The day before, Prabowo had picked former Coordinating Minister for Economics [Hatta Rajasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatta_Rajasa "Hatta Rajasa") as his vice-presidential running mate.[\[117\]](#ref-119)

On 22 July 2014, the day that the KPU was due to announce its official tally, Prabowo withdrew from the race after having insisted on his victory since the initial quick counts were released, although the majority showed Jokowi ahead. He attributed this withdrawal to Indonesia "failing in its duty to democracy" because of "massive cheating that is structured and systematic,"[\[118\]](#ref-SMH1) and stated that he and Hatta "exercise our constitutional right to reject the presidential election and declare it unconstitutional".[\[119\]](#ref-Tolan-2014) His speech, aired live, implied he would challenge the results in the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia "Constitutional Court of Indonesia").[\[118\]](#ref-SMH1) Later reports indicated confusion over whether Prabowo had resigned from the election or simply rejected the count.[\[120\]](#ref-Manurung-2014)

According to Douglas Ramage of the Jakarta-based Bower's Asia Group, this was the first time since _[Reformasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Suharto_era_in_Indonesia "Post-Suharto era in Indonesia")_ began in 1998 that the legitimacy of an election was questioned; he declared the country was entering "uncharted territory."[\[119\]](#ref-Tolan-2014) The legality of a Prabowo challenge is questionable, as – if he withdrew – he is no longer considered a presidential candidate.[\[119\]](#ref-Tolan-2014) If he can make the challenge, according to _[The Jakarta Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jakarta_Post "The Jakarta Post")_, the gap between the two is sufficient to make such a challenge difficult.[\[121\]](#ref-JP1) Under the presidential election law, Prabowo could face up to six years in prison and a 100billion rupiah (US$10million) fine for withdrawing.[\[118\]](#ref-SMH1)[\[119\]](#ref-Tolan-2014) Later that evening, Joko Widodo was officially announced as president and began to receive congratulations from world leaders.[\[122\]](#ref-NYT)

Following the announcement, the value of the [Indonesian rupiah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_rupiah "Indonesian rupiah") dropped by 0.3%, and the [JSX Composite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JSX_Composite "JSX Composite") fell by 0.9%.[\[120\]](#ref-Manurung-2014) Observers denied Prabowo's allegations of cheating, finding that the elections were "generally fair and free;" Maswadi Rauf of the [University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indonesia "University of Indonesia") stated that there was "no sign of significant fraud," and that Prabowo's withdrawal simply reflected "the real attitudes of the elite, who are not yet ready to accept losing."[\[123\]](#ref-125) On 21 August 2014, the Indonesian Constitutional Court rejected his claim of fraud, confirming his election loss.[\[124\]](#ref-126)

### 2019 general election

See also: [Prabowo Subianto 2019 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto_2019_presidential_campaign "Prabowo Subianto 2019 presidential campaign")

On 12 April 2018, Prabowo announced he would contest the [2019 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indonesian_general_election "2019 Indonesian general election") if he could obtain sufficient support from other political parties.[\[125\]](#ref-127) Indonesian media had speculated on whether Prabowo would become a presidential candidate or a "king-maker" giving his support to another candidate.[\[126\]](#ref-128) Prabowo's brother Hashim in March 2018 said health and logistical factors had to be considered before the party announces a presidential candidate.[\[127\]](#ref-129)

In April 2018, [John McBeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McBeth "John McBeth") reported Maritime Coordinating Minister [Luhut Binsar Pandjaitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhut_Binsar_Pandjaitan "Luhut Binsar Pandjaitan") had held a series of meetings with Prabowo, culminating in the proposal of a joint Jokowi-Prabowo ticket for the 2019 election. Luhut reportedly lost his enthusiasm after Prabowo allegedly said he would want to be in charge of the military and seven seats in any new cabinet.[\[128\]](#ref-130) Fadli Zon denied Luhut and Prabowo had discussed politics, claiming they merely spoke about Europe's move to limit imports of Indonesian [palm oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_oil "Palm oil"). Gerindra official Andre Rosiade also dismissed the report as a hoax.[\[129\]](#ref-131)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Jokowi_selfie_with_Prabowo_and_reporters.jpg/500px-Jokowi_selfie_with_Prabowo_and_reporters.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jokowi_selfie_with_Prabowo_and_reporters.jpg) Prabowo and former Indonesian President [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo "Joko Widodo") on 11 October 2019

On 10 August 2018, Prabowo registered at the KPU office for the 2019 presidential election with [Sandiaga Uno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandiaga_Uno "Sandiaga Uno") as his running mate and with the support of Gerindra, PKS, PAN, the Democratic Party and [Berkarya Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkarya_Party "Berkarya Party").[\[10\]](#ref-PrabowoSandiaga) The [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(Indonesia) "Democratic Party (Indonesia)") had wanted Prabowo to choose [Agus Harimurti Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agus_Harimurti_Yudhoyono "Agus Harimurti Yudhoyono") as his running mate.[\[130\]](#ref-132) Following the election, 'quick counts' conducted at polling stations by independent institutions authorised by the government indicated Jokowi had won by a margin of about 10%, but Prabowo claimed victory, insisting a real count by his side showed he received 62% of the vote.[\[131\]](#ref-133) His unsubstantiated claims of widespread cheating prompted his supporters to stage protests in Jakarta, resulting in riots that left eight people dead and 737 injured.[\[132\]](#ref-134)[\[133\]](#ref-135) The Constitutional Court in June 2019 unanimously rejected Prabowo's appeal against the election result.[\[134\]](#ref-136)

On 14 July 2019, Prabowo finally conceded to Jokowi in a train ride at [Jakarta MRT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta_MRT "Jakarta MRT"), congratulated Jokowi and apologised for criticising him during the campaign trail much to the criticism of his upset supporters who expressed that Prabowo should remain as part of the opposition.[\[135\]](#ref-137)[\[136\]](#ref-138) His own party Gerindra eventually joined Jokowi's [Onward Indonesia Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onward_Indonesia_Coalition "Onward Indonesia Coalition") on 21 October 2019 with Prabowo himself appointed [Minister of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Defense (Indonesia)") on 23 October 2019, therefore joining Jokowi's cabinet.[\[137\]](#ref-:0)[\[138\]](#ref-140)

### 2024 general election

See also: [Prabowo Subianto 2024 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto_2024_presidential_campaign "Prabowo Subianto 2024 presidential campaign")

On 7 January 2023, Prabowo launched his third presidential campaign for the [2024 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_general_election "2024 Indonesian general election").[\[139\]](#ref-141) He is running as president, together with [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka "Gibran Rakabuming Raka") (the eldest son of [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo "Joko Widodo"), the former President of Indonesia), as his vice-presidential candidate. Both he and Gibran registered at the KPU office on 25 October 2023 with the support of Gerindra, Golkar, PAN, PBB, the Democratic Party, [Gelora Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gelora_Party "Gelora Party"), [Indonesian Solidarity Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Solidarity_Party "Indonesian Solidarity Party") (PSI) and [Garuda Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garuda_Party "Garuda Party").[\[140\]](#ref-142) Both he and Gibran's candidacies were challenged in the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia "Constitutional Court of Indonesia") due to age requirements. In Prabowo's case, a lawsuit was filed against him due to his old age (Prabowo was 71 at the time of announcing his candidacy) and having announced his candidacy for the third time.[\[141\]](#ref-143) The Supreme Court ruled to reject the lawsuit.[\[142\]](#ref-144)

In November 2023, Prabowo called for "rebalancing" where, in the past decades, Indonesia looked to the West; now they should learn from the East like [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia-China_relations "Indonesia-China relations"), [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia-India_relations "Indonesia-India relations"), [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia-Japan_relations "Indonesia-Japan relations") and [South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia-South_Korea_relations "Indonesia-South Korea relations").[\[143\]](#ref-145) Prabowo entertained the idea of Indonesia joining [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS") if it is advantageous for Indonesia's economy, citing BRICS' nature as an economic bloc, not a geopolitical one.[\[144\]](#ref-146)

On 14 February 2024, in an unofficial report, Prabowo succeeded in leading three voting lines by getting 58% of the vote.[\[145\]](#ref-Wee-2024) Prabowo went on to win the quick count conducted by all surveys and called for unity as he pledged to be president for all Indonesians and will form a government that will be led by "the best sons and daughters of Indonesia."[\[146\]](#ref-:4) Despite this, he also told his supporters to calmly but cautiously wait for the official results from KPU.[\[146\]](#ref-:4)

On 20 March 2024, the [General Elections Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Elections_Commission_(Indonesia) "General Elections Commission (Indonesia)") (KPU) announced Prabowo's victory, having received over 96 million votes. Prabowo and his vice-presidential candidate, Gibran Rakabuming, they were sworn in on 20 October 2024.[\[147\]](#ref-149)

On 22 April 2024, the Constitutional Court rejected all legal challenges against the results of the presidential election, allowing the KPU to declare the Prabowo-Gibran tandem the winner.[\[148\]](#ref-150)[\[149\]](#ref-151)[\[150\]](#ref-152)

## Minister of Defence (2019–2024)

### Appointment

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Menteri_Pertahanan_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg/500px-Menteri_Pertahanan_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Menteri_Pertahanan_Prabowo_Subianto.jpg) Official portrait of Prabowo as Minister of Defence, 2019[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Inauguration_of_Prabowo_Subianto_as_Minister_of_Defence%2C_2019.jpg/960px-Inauguration_of_Prabowo_Subianto_as_Minister_of_Defence%2C_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Inauguration_of_Prabowo_Subianto_as_Minister_of_Defence,_2019.jpg) Inauguration of Prabowo as Minister of Defence, 2019

On 23 October 2019, Prabowo was inaugurated as Indonesia's Minister of Defence by president Joko Widodo.[\[137\]](#ref-:0) He was aided by [Sakti Wahyu Trenggono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakti_Wahyu_Trenggono "Sakti Wahyu Trenggono") as Deputy Minister of Defence. According to Jokowi, Prabowo got the job because "Indeed, he has a lot of experience there."[\[151\]](#ref-153) Jokowi clarified his decision to appoint Prabowo was to build "democracy based on mutual cooperation" and said it is for the good of the country,[\[152\]](#ref-154) while Prabowo said he express his desire to serve and his decision to accept the appointment is to preserve national unity.[\[153\]](#ref-155)

### Military

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Prabowo_periksa_calon_anggota_Komponen_Cadangan.jpg/500px-Prabowo_periksa_calon_anggota_Komponen_Cadangan.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prabowo_periksa_calon_anggota_Komponen_Cadangan.jpg) Prabowo inspecting Indonesian Reserve soldiers in October 2021

Shortly after his inauguration, Prabowo began advocating for a "total people's war" doctrine for Indonesia's national defence.[\[154\]](#ref-156) In realisation to this, he formed the [Reserve Component](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Reserve_Component "Indonesian National Armed Forces Reserve Component") on 7 October 2021 and appointed TV presenter [Deddy Corbuzier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deddy_Corbuzier "Deddy Corbuzier") as the reserve component ambassador to boost recruitment.[\[155\]](#ref-157)

Considering that some of the TNI's equipment is old, Prabowo has made efforts to continue modernising military capabilities. He believes that long-term planning, based on the assumption that the defence budget is fixed at 0.8% of Indonesia's GDP, will enable Indonesia to purchase the most advanced weapons and build a domestic defence industry.[\[156\]](#ref-158) The plan, which emerged to the public not long after the sinking of the [KRI _Nanggala_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KRI_Nanggala_(402) "KRI Nanggala (402)") submarine, became a hot debate because the proposed figure reached [Rp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_rupiah "Indonesian rupiah") 1,700 trillion (Rp 1.7 quadrillion) or Rp 68 trillion per year.[\[157\]](#ref-159)

Prabowo utilised the certainty of the defence budget to acquire advanced weaponry that the TNI had never possessed. He plans to significantly expand Indonesia's domestic capacity to manufacture ammunition, as the country's current production capability is only 450 million rounds per year, despite an annual demand of one billion rounds.[\[158\]](#ref-160) For the [Indonesian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Air_Force "Indonesian Air Force"), Prabowo is looking to strengthen Indonesia's military by acquiring newer fighter aircraft such as [General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon "General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcon") and [Dassault Rafales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Rafale "Dassault Rafale") from the US and France[\[159\]](#ref-161) while in the same time buying new transport aircraft such as [Airbus A400M Atlas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_A400M_Atlas "Airbus A400M Atlas") and newer [Lockheed Martin C-130J Super Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_C-130J_Super_Hercules "Lockheed Martin C-130J Super Hercules"). For the [Indonesian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Navy "Indonesian Navy"), Prabowo signed contracts for newer surface combatants such as [Arrowhead 140](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_31_frigate "Type 31 frigate") and [FREMM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FREMM_multipurpose_frigate "FREMM multipurpose frigate")[\[160\]](#ref-162)[\[161\]](#ref-163) while exploring possible local production on the [Scorpene class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorp%C3%A8ne-class_submarine "Scorpène-class submarine") submarines under [PAL Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PAL_Indonesia "PAL Indonesia").[\[162\]](#ref-164) He also ordered 500 [Pindad Maung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindad_Maung "Pindad Maung") light utility vehicles in 2020.[\[163\]](#ref-165)

He also led renegotiation attempts regarding on the payment of the [KAI KF-21 Boramae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_KF-21_Boramae "KAI KF-21 Boramae") fighter program after his predecessor, [Ryamizard Ryacudu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryamizard_Ryacudu "Ryamizard Ryacudu") stalled the payment of Indonesia's shares in the program.[\[164\]](#ref-166) He later attended the launch of the aircraft prototype together with South Korean President [Moon Jae-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Jae-in "Moon Jae-in") representing Indonesia.[\[165\]](#ref-167)

On 20 April 2022, together with [Minister of State Owned Enterprise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_State_Owned_Enterprises_(Indonesia) "Minister of State Owned Enterprises (Indonesia)") [Erick Thohir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erick_Thohir "Erick Thohir"), Prabowo strengthened the Indonesian defence industry by consolidating PT Len, [Pindad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindad "Pindad"), [PAL Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PAL_Indonesia "PAL Indonesia"), [Indonesian Aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Aerospace "Indonesian Aerospace") and PT Dahana into a single holding, [Defend ID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defend_ID "Defend ID").[\[166\]](#ref-168)

### Food estate program

In 2020, Prabowo was appointed by Jokowi to spearhead the national food estate program. Jokowi's reasoning for Prabowo's appointment was that as Minister of Defence, Prabowo also plays important role on carrying out strategic projects such as food storage. The development of food barns in [Central Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Kalimantan "Central Kalimantan") began in mid-2020, utilising existing rice fields covering an area of around 30,000 hectares, with 10,000 hectares located in [Pulang Pisau Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulang_Pisau_Regency "Pulang Pisau Regency") and 20,000 hectares in [Kapuas Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapuas_Regency "Kapuas Regency"). In 2021, this project will be expanded to 44,135 hectares.[\[167\]](#ref-:8) Although Prabowo was selected as the project leader, he emphasise that the [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Defense (Indonesia)") will only play a supporting role in the food estate work as the [Minister of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Agriculture_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Agriculture (Indonesia)") will play the leading role, indicating that cross-sector collaboration is the key to the success of this project. This reflects the synergy between the agricultural and defence sectors in an effort to achieve sustainability and national food security.[\[167\]](#ref-:8)

### Defence Cooperation

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Prabowo_bertemu_Menhan_UAE_disaksikan_Presiden_Jokowi.jpg/500px-Prabowo_bertemu_Menhan_UAE_disaksikan_Presiden_Jokowi.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prabowo_bertemu_Menhan_UAE_disaksikan_Presiden_Jokowi.jpg) Prabowo meeting with [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates "United Arab Emirates") defence minister [Mohammed bin Rashid Al Maktoum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Rashid_Al_Maktoum "Mohammed bin Rashid Al Maktoum") in July 2022

In his first 100 days as Minister of Defence, Prabowo visited 8 countries to meet the Ministers of Defence of [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(Malaysia) "Minister of Defence (Malaysia)"), [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(Thailand) "Minister of Defence (Thailand)"), Turkey, China, Japan, the Philippines, and France to strengthen military relations.[\[168\]](#ref-170)[\[169\]](#ref-171)

Prabowo also made efforts to help state owned defence enterprises to export their products overseas. He succeeded in helping [Indonesian Aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Aerospace "Indonesian Aerospace") export [CN-235](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CASA/IPTN_CN-235 "CASA/IPTN CN-235") aircraft to [Senegal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senegal "Senegal") and export [NC-212i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CASA_C-212_Aviocar "CASA C-212 Aviocar") aircraft to [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand "Thailand"),[\[170\]](#ref-172) as well as helping [PAL Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PAL_Indonesia "PAL Indonesia") export [landing platform dock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibious_transport_dock "Amphibious transport dock")-type warships to the [Philippine Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Navy "Philippine Navy").[\[171\]](#ref-173)

In August 2024, Prabowo and Australian Prime Minister [Anthony Albanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Albanese "Anthony Albanese") announced a new defence cooperation agreement to strengthen [Australia–Indonesia security ties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Indonesia_Security_Agreements "Australia–Indonesia Security Agreements").[\[172\]](#ref-174)

#### South China Sea

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Courtesy_Call_from_President-elect_Prabowo_of_Indonesia_to_PM_Kishida_%282024%29_02.jpg/500px-Courtesy_Call_from_President-elect_Prabowo_of_Indonesia_to_PM_Kishida_%282024%29_02.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Courtesy_Call_from_President-elect_Prabowo_of_Indonesia_to_PM_Kishida_(2024)_02.jpg) Prabowo with Japanese prime minister [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida "Fumio Kishida") in April 2024

Following an incident in late 2019 where Chinese vessels violated the Indonesian [EEZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_Economic_Zone "Exclusive Economic Zone") off the [Natuna Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natuna_Islands "Natuna Islands"), Prabowo called for a cautious response, referring to China as a "friendly nation".[\[173\]](#ref-175) He also ordered the deployment of additional navy vessels in the region in response to the incident.[\[174\]](#ref-176) Despite the controversy of his inaction in Natuna Island in contrast to his fiery speeches about foreign powers during the 2019 elections,[\[175\]](#ref-177) a poll by Indo Barometer on early January shows that Prabowo is the most popular minister in Jokowi's cabinet.[\[176\]](#ref-178)

When asked about the situation on the [South China Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Sea "South China Sea") in a session at IISS Shangri-La Dialogue on 14 June 2022, Prabowo answered that Indonesia respects the interest of all countries that are involved in the region. He then quoted [Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela "Nelson Mandela") that "your enemies must not be our enemies too".[\[177\]](#ref-179) In reaction to the formation of [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS "AUKUS") and Australian plans to acquire [nuclear submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SSN-AUKUS "SSN-AUKUS"), Prabowo thinks that the alliance is made on basis of defence and it was beneath the sovereign rights of Australia, UK, and USA.[\[178\]](#ref-180)

[US Department of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Defense "United States Department of Defense") said both Secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin "Lloyd Austin") and Minister Prabowo viewed China's aggression in the South China Sea as inconsistent to [international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law "International law"). This statement was then refuted by Spokesperson of the Foreign Ministry [Wang Wenbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Wenbin "Wang Wenbin") as a lie, claiming Prabowo never said that when Chinese embassy contacted the press office of the [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Defense (Indonesia)").[\[179\]](#ref-181) Prabowo himself later refuted the US statement, stating there was no joint statement nor a press conference regarding to this matter and states that Indonesia is in good terms with China, US, and Russia and respects their interests, insisting Indonesia's [non-aligned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement "Non-Aligned Movement") stances.[\[180\]](#ref-182)

During the 3rd presidential debate of the [2024 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_general_election "2024 Indonesian general election"), Prabowo argued that the situation in the region underlines the needs of a strong military. He states that the military needs more patrol platform and satellites to defend the nation from outside threats.[\[181\]](#ref-183)

#### United States

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Secretary_of_Defense_Lloyd_Austin_walks_with_Indonesian_Defense_Minister_Prabowo_Subianto_during_a_bilateral_exchange_at_the_Pentagon_on_August_24%2C_2023_-_230824-D-PM193-1179.jpg/500px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Secretary_of_Defense_Lloyd_Austin_walks_with_Indonesian_Defense_Minister_Prabowo_Subianto_during_a_bilateral_exchange_at_the_Pentagon_on_August_24,_2023_-_230824-D-PM193-1179.jpg) Prabowo with US secretary of defence [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin "Lloyd Austin") in August 2023[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Indonesian_Defense_Minister_Prabowo_Subianto_during_a_bilateral_exchange_at_The_Pentagon_on_August_24%2C_2023_-_230824-D-PM193-1190_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-Indonesian_Defense_Minister_Prabowo_Subianto_during_a_bilateral_exchange_at_The_Pentagon_on_August_24%2C_2023_-_230824-D-PM193-1190_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indonesian_Defense_Minister_Prabowo_Subianto_during_a_bilateral_exchange_at_The_Pentagon_on_August_24,_2023_-_230824-D-PM193-1190_(cropped).jpg) Prabowo during a bilateral exchange at [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon "The Pentagon") on 24 August 2023

In October 2020, Prabowo visited the United States despite his previous ban from entering the country, in his capacity as Minister of Defence after being invited by his US counterpart [Mark Esper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Esper "Mark Esper") and a visa was issued for him. Several human rights organisations, including [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International"), had previously called for the Trump administration to cancel the visit.[\[182\]](#ref-184) From his frequent foreign visit overtures such as in the United States, Prabowo succeeded in convincing the [United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress") to approve the sale of the advanced [F-15EX Eagle II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_F-15EX_Eagle_II "Boeing F-15EX Eagle II") [supersonic fighter aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fighter_aircraft "Fighter aircraft") to Indonesia.[\[183\]](#ref-185)

#### Russo-Ukrainian War

On 3 June 2023, Prabowo proposed a [multi-point peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Peace negotiations in the Russian invasion of Ukraine") to end the ongoing [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Russian invasion of Ukraine") at [IISS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Strategic_Studies "International Institute for Strategic Studies") [Shangri-La Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shangri-La_Dialogue "Shangri-La Dialogue") in Singapore.[\[184\]](#ref-:5)[\[185\]](#ref-187) His proposal includes the following.[\[184\]](#ref-:5)

1. Immediate cessation of hostilities through cease-fire
2. Establishment of demilitarised zones by withdrawing 15 kilometres from their forward positions
3. UN referendums on disputed territories

Prabowo's proposal was dismissed outright by Ukrainian defence minister [Oleksii Reznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksii_Reznikov "Oleksii Reznikov"), stating that the proposal was "a Russian plan, not an Indonesian plan".[\[186\]](#ref-:6) This proposal was also criticised by EU foreign policy chief [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell "Josep Borrell"), saying that there must be a "just peace" not "peace of surrender".[\[186\]](#ref-:6) It was also criticised by [PDI-P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle "Indonesian Democratic Party of Struggle") as Secretary General [Hasto Kristiyanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasto_Kristiyanto "Hasto Kristiyanto") said Indonesia does not need a leader who gives advice that deviates from the principle of "free and active" diplomacy.[\[187\]](#ref-189) However, his plan was well received by China, with former Chinese ambassador to the United States [Cui Tiankai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cui_Tiankai "Cui Tiankai") expressing appreciation for Prabowo's efforts while criticising [the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_world "Western world") for mismanaging their own security situation.[\[188\]](#ref-:7) Prabowo was later called by Jokowi for clarifications on the peace plan as Indonesia had officially condemned Russia for the invasion.[\[188\]](#ref-:7)[\[189\]](#ref-191) Jokowi then clarified that Prabowo's peace plan as "okay" because it was just a suggestion in an open dialogue and did not officially reflect Indonesia's stance.[\[190\]](#ref-192)

On 2 June 2024, Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") met Prabowo on the sidelines of IISS Shangri-La Dialogue in Singapore and invited him to attend the [June 2024 Ukraine peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit "June 2024 Ukraine peace summit") at [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva "Geneva"), hoping for his attendance.[\[191\]](#ref-193) In response to this invitation, Prabowo said he could not confirm his presence to the June summit and he had to discuss with President Jokowi, [Minister of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Indonesia)") [Retno Marsudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retno_Marsudi "Retno Marsudi") and [Coordinating Minister for Political, Legal, and Security Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinating_Ministry_for_Political,_Legal,_and_Security_Affairs "Coordinating Ministry for Political, Legal, and Security Affairs") [Hadi Tjahjanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadi_Tjahjanto "Hadi Tjahjanto") on Indonesia's official stance in attending the summit.[\[192\]](#ref-194) Prabowo told Zelenskyy that "Many countries feel that in a peace summit all elements must be present, especially Russia".[\[193\]](#ref-195) Prabowo offered Indonesia's solution to Zelenskyy which he seemed not to accept Indonesia's proposal on ceasefire and Prabowo is keen to continue finding a solution. Prabowo also offered Zelenskyy Indonesia's good service in any avenues to find solutions for peace between Russia and Ukraine.[\[194\]](#ref-196)

In July 2024, Prabowo held talks in Moscow with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin") and Defence Minister [Andrey Belousov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Belousov "Andrey Belousov").[\[195\]](#ref-197) Belousov called Indonesia one of Russia's key partners in the Asia-Pacific region.[\[196\]](#ref-198)

#### Gaza-Israel conflict

During the [Israel-Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war") in 2023, Prabowo called out his disappointment with the [Western countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_world "Western world") who seemed silent when they saw civilians and children becoming victims of war and blasted the west for a perceived double standards on [human rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights "Human rights"). He has donated Rp. 5 billion of his personal wealth to the Palestinian people[\[197\]](#ref-199) and joined President Jokowi, Secretary of State [Pratikno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratikno "Pratikno"), [Minister of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Indonesia)") [Retno Marsudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retno_Marsudi "Retno Marsudi") and Ambassador to Indonesia from Palestine [Zuhair Al-Shun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuhair_Al-Shun "Zuhair Al-Shun") in sending off 51.5 tonnes of aid delivered by the Indonesian Air Force.[\[198\]](#ref-200) He had also communicated with the Egyptian defence minister [Mohamed Ahmad Zaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Ahmed_Zaki "Mohamed Ahmed Zaki") to allow the passage of the Indonesian Navy hospital ship [KRI _dr. Radjiman Wedyodiningrat_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KRI_dr._Radjiman_Wedyodiningrat "KRI dr. Radjiman Wedyodiningrat") to dock near [El Arish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arish "Arish"), [Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula "Sinai Peninsula") in order to deliver further aid for Palestine.[\[199\]](#ref-201)
